# Candidate policy requirements inventory

Local extraction aid for the first [deployment checklist item](checklist.md). Not approved policy or evidence of an implemented engine.
See [review instructions](README.md#requirements-inventory). Edit only review fields; add missed claims using MANUAL IDs. Absent claims are historical, never applicable requirements.
Numeric HTML entities preserve exact source wording, including newlines and Markdown characters. No generation date is a review date.

## Scanned sources

File hashes identify the scanned source revision, not approval. Changed context still requires human review even when a quoted claim is unchanged.

Available to researchers via internal mirror. ===" | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-f6bd652b35a1d60fde44-1-1 | [src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx](../src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx#L93) | 93 | # TRUSTED RESEARCH ENVIRONMENT (TRE) CLINICAL DATA GOVERNANCE CHARTER&#10;*Standard Operating Protocol &#38; Statutory Compliance Specification*&#10;*Document Reference: TRE-PAC-GOV-2026-V4 &#124; Approved by Trust Board &#38; Caldicott Guardian*&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 1. Statutory Mandate &#38; Legal Framework&#10;This Trusted Research Environment (TRE) operates in strict accordance with UK health data legislation:&#10;- **UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR)**:&#10;  - Article 6(1)(e): Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 9(2)(j): Processing of special category health data is necessary for archiving and scientific research purposes in the public interest.&#10;- **Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA 2018)**: Part 2 and Section 19 safeguards for scientific research.&#10;- **National Health Service Act 2006 (Section 251)**: Statutory exemption granted on advice of the Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG) permitting processing of patient data without consent for essential medical research.&#10;- **Health and Social Care Act 2012**: Mandatory statutory directions on secure health data custody.&#10;- **Common Law Duty of Confidentiality (CLDC)**: Enforced via the Eight Caldicott Principles.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 2. Institutional Roles &#38; Oversight Structure&#10;- **Caldicott Guardian**: Responsible for championing confidentiality and reviewing all Tier 3 data access requests.&#10;- **Senior Information Risk Owner (SIRO)**: Board-level accountability for technical and cyber risks across the cloud enclave.&#10;- **Data Protection Officer (DPO)**: Conducts Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIA) and audits compliance with UK GDPR.&#10;- **Research Ethics Committee (REC)**: Independent health ethics body reviewing all research questions for demonstrable public benefit.&#10;- **System-Level Security Custodian**: Technical lead maintaining zero-egress VPC controls and Open Policy Agent rule compilation.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 3. The Five Safes Operational Standard&#10;Every project instantiated within the TRE must mathematically satisfy the Five Safes framework:&#10;1. **Safe People**:&#10;   - Mandatory institutional accreditation with verified university or NHS employment.&#10;   - Active, annual NHS Information Governance (IG) training certification.&#10;   - Signed binding Data User Agreements with legal penalties for misuse.&#10;2. **Safe Projects**:&#10;   - Active approval letter from an accredited NHS Research Ethics Committee (REC).&#10;   - Valid Section 251 CAG exemption or explicit patient consent.&#10;   - Documented research protocol demonstrating public health benefit.&#10;3. **Safe Settings**:&#10;   - Isolated Virtual Private Cloud (VPC) with zero external internet routing.&#10;   - Inbound access restricted to multi-factor authenticated (MFA) virtual desktops and notebooks.&#10;   - Prohibition of local disk copying, clipboard exfiltration, or unauthorized external API connections.&#10;4. **Safe Data**:&#10;   - Permanent redaction of direct identifiers (NHS number, patient names, full postcodes, full DOBs).&#10;   - Partitioning into hospital operational tiers (Tier 1 Aggregate, Tier 2 Pseudonymised, Tier 3 Section 251 Sensitive).&#10;   - Minimization: researchers receive only approved study columns and demographic slices.&#10;5. **Safe Outputs**:&#10;   - Compulsory digital airlock review on all summary tables, regression outputs, and machine learning models.&#10;   - Enforced Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) thresholds prior to release.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 4. Quantitative Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) Protocol&#10;All analytical outputs submitted to the airlock must conform to national standards (ONS and SAIL Databank):&#10;- **Primary Cell Suppression ($k &#92;&#92;ge 10$)**:&#10;  - Any frequency count or cell in a summary table representing fewer than 10 individuals ($k &#60; 10$) must be suppressed or grouped.&#10;- **Dominance Rule ($(1, 80&#92;&#92;%)$ or $n,k$ rule)**:&#10;  - If a single patient or clinic accounts for more than 80% of an aggregated metric (e.g. expenditure or bed days), the cell must be masked to prevent attribute disclosure.&#10;- **Secondary (Residual) Suppression**:&#10;  - Additional non-sensitive cells must be suppressed to ensure that suppressed cells cannot be deduced by subtracting remaining cells from row or column totals.&#10;- **Tabular Aggregation Limits**:&#10;  - Maxima, minima, and percentiles (p0, p100) are prohibited; interquartile ranges and median figures must be used.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 5. Artificial Intelligence &#38; Autonomous Analytics Agent Policy&#10;When autonomous LLMs or code assistants operate within the TRE:&#10;- Workloads are classified under the **EU AI Act** (Limited Risk for code synthesis; High Risk for diagnostic inference).&#10;- Agent execution is strictly governed by **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** tool boundaries:&#10;  - &#92;&#96;mcp:ExecuteSQL&#92;&#96;: Barred from executing DDL/DML (DROP, ALTER, UPDATE, INSERT); limited to read-only &#92;&#96;SELECT&#92;&#96; with row caps (max 500 rows).&#10;  - &#92;&#96;mcp:ExportSummary&#92;&#96;: Forced through automated SDC shape verification prior to human airlock review.&#10;  - Zero external network egress: LLMs cannot communicate with external model servers or leak data outside the enclave.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 6. Automated Policy-as-Code Technical Architecture&#10;Governance is not maintained on paper; it is compiled into deterministic runtime enforcement code:&#10;1. **Semantic Ontology (&#92;&#96;ontology/*.ttl&#92;&#96;)**: Encodes Five Safes, hospital tiers, and Caldicott principles in OWL 2 RDF.&#10;2. **Closed-World Validation (&#92;&#96;ontology/shapes/*.ttl&#92;&#96;)**: Transforms the Open-World Assumption into deterministic Closed-World SHACL shapes.&#10;3. **Automated Compiler (&#92;&#96;compiler/rdf_to_rego.py&#92;&#96;)**: Compiles semantic shapes into Open Policy Agent (OPA) Rego rules.&#10;4. **Policy Enforcement Points (PEP)**: Kubernetes admission controllers intercept compute pod provisioning in microseconds.&#10;5. **Cryptographic Provenance**: Every admission and airlock decision emits a W3C PROV-O RDF triple to an append-only audit ledger.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 7. Public Transparency, Patient Opt-Out, and FOI Procedures&#10;- **National Data Opt-Out**: Respected in full. Patients who have opted out of secondary research have their records excluded prior to enclave ingestion.&#10;- **Freedom of Information (FOI)**: Under the FOI Act 2000, this Governance Charter and all compiled policy hashes are published openly.&#10;- **Audit Lineage Review**: Trust boards and external auditors receive automated monthly provenance digests generated via &#92;&#96;compiler/generate_docs.py&#92;&#96;.&#10;&#10;*Published by the Clinical Information Governance Board.* | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-166f6e999db425d33833-1-1 | [src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx](../src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx#L244) | 244 | # TRUSTED RESEARCH ENVIRONMENT (TRE) CLINICAL DATA GOVERNANCE CHARTER&#10;*Standard Operating Protocol &#38; Statutory Compliance Specification*&#10;*Document Reference: TRE-PAC-GOV-2026-V4 &#124; Approved by Trust Board &#38; Caldicott Guardian*&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 1. Statutory Mandate &#38; Legal Framework&#10;This platform operates in strict accordance with UK health data legislation and international standards:&#10;- **UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR)**:&#10;  - Article 6(1)(e): Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 9(2)(j): Processing of special category health data is necessary for archiving and scientific research purposes in the public interest.&#10;- **Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA 2018)**: Part 2 and Section 19 safeguards for scientific research.&#10;- **National Health Service Act 2006 (Section 251)**: Statutory exemption granted on advice of the Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG).&#10;- **ISO/IEC 42001:2023**: Artificial Intelligence Management System (AIMS) governance.&#10;- **DCB0129 &#38; DCB0160**: NHS Clinical Risk Management for Health Software and Deployment.&#10;- **UK MDR 2002 &#38; MHRA SaMD**: Software as a Medical Device risk control and classification.&#10;- **Common Law Duty of Confidentiality (CLDC)**: Enforced via the Eight Caldicott Principles.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 2. Institutional Roles &#38; Oversight Structure&#10;- **Caldicott Guardian**: Responsible for championing confidentiality and reviewing all Tier 3 data access requests.&#10;- **Senior Information Risk Owner (SIRO)**: Board-level accountability for technical and cyber risks across the cloud enclave.&#10;- **Clinical Safety Officer (CSO)**: Registered clinician responsible for DCB0129 Hazard Logs and Safety Cases.&#10;- **Data Protection Officer (DPO)**: Conducts Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIA) and audits compliance with UK GDPR.&#10;- **Research Ethics Committee (REC)**: Independent health ethics body reviewing all research questions for demonstrable public benefit.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 3. The Seawater Tidal Model &#38; Five Safes&#10;- **Flood Tide (Ingress)**: Partitioned EHR and genomic data ingest through cryptographically audited pipelines.&#10;- **Safe Setting**: Airgapped digital safe room; no USB copying, no local downloads, zero outbound internet.&#10;- **Ebb Tide (Egress)**: Safe outputs pass through mathematical airlocks enforcing cell suppression ($k &#92;&#92;ge 10$) and dominance masking.&#10;&#10;*Published by the Clinical Information Governance Board.* | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
| C-1a6b1fb7deeba97fde64-1-1 | [src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx](../src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx#L244) | 244 | # PUBLIC INTEREST DATA GOVERNANCE &#38; POPULATION RESEARCH CHARTER&#10;*Standard Operating Protocol &#38; Statutory Compliance Specification for Data Analysis &#38; Intelligence Platforms*&#10;*Document Reference: WAVEBOUND-PAC-GOV-2026-V4 &#124; Authorized by the Data Governance &#38; Ethics Oversight Committee*&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 1. Purpose &#38; Scope&#10;This Charter establishes the mandatory governance policy, ethical safeguards, and machine-executable controls for modern data analysis and intelligence platforms (including Trusted Research Environments [TREs], Secure Data Environments [SDEs], and AI sandbox enclaves).&#10;&#10;The platform supports high-impact research across population data science, epidemiology, public health, demographic analysis, social statistics, and approved commercial health life-sciences partnerships. All analytical workloads operate under an overarching principle: data subjects and citizens must remain completely protected against individual re-identification, surveillance, or unauthorized exploitation.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 2. Statutory Mandates &#38; Legal Processing Gateways&#10;Processing across this platform is authorized and governed under statutory legislation:&#10;- **Digital Economy Act 2017 (Part 5, Chapter 5: Research)**:&#10;  - Section 64: Disclosure of public authority data for research in the public interest.&#10;  - Section 70 &#38; 71: Statutory Code of Practice and accreditation of processors, environments, and researchers under UK Statistics Authority (UKSA) oversight.&#10;  - Section 67: Strict criminal penalties for unlawful or unauthorized disclosure of identifying data.&#10;- **UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR)**:&#10;  - Article 5: Principles of data minimization, purpose limitation, integrity, and confidentiality.&#10;  - Article 6(1)(e): Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 9(2)(j): Processing of special category data is lawful for archiving and scientific research in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 89(1): Technical and organizational safeguards for research and statistical analysis.&#10;- **Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA 2018)**: Part 2 statutory provisions and Section 19 research safeguards.&#10;- **Statistics and Registration Service Act 2007 (SRSA 2007)**: Authorizing statistical research and linkage under public interest mandates.&#10;- **National Health Service Act 2006 (Section 251)**: Statutory exemption granted on advice of the Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG) where clinical data linkage is required without consent.&#10;- **Common Law Duty of Confidentiality &#38; The Eight Caldicott Principles**: Ensuring that duty to share for public benefit is balanced with strict confidentiality.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 3. Standards Architecture &#38; Quality Frameworks&#10;The platform compiles and verifies compliance against national and international standards:&#10;- **ISO/IEC 42001:2023**: Artificial Intelligence Management System (AIMS) governing predictive models and in-enclave machine learning.&#10;- **ISO/IEC 27001:2022 &#38; ISO/IEC 27701:2019**: Information Security and Privacy Information Management Systems (ISMS/PIMS).&#10;- **ISO/IEC 5259 Series**: Data quality and bias minimization standards for machine learning and analytical pipelines.&#10;- **DCB0129 &#38; DCB0160**: Statutory clinical risk management standards for health software manufacturers and deploying health organizations.&#10;- **UK Medical Devices Regulations 2002 &#38; MHRA SaMD**: Design and risk controls for Software as a Medical Device developed within enclaves.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 4. Operational Five Safes Framework&#10;Every analytical project is partitioned and controlled across the Five Safes:&#10;&#10;### 4.1 Safe People&#10;- Mandatory accreditation by statutory bodies (e.g., UKSA Research Accreditation Panel [RAP] or equivalent).&#10;- Verified institutional identity and background checks.&#10;- Annual certification in Information Governance (IG), data protection, and disclosure control methods.&#10;&#10;### 4.2 Safe Projects&#10;- Demonstrable public interest benefit test evaluated by an independent review committee.&#10;- Validated ethics clearances (NHS REC, university ethics, or statutory research panels).&#10;- Clear purpose limitation: use of data is restricted exclusively to the approved research question.&#10;- Strict prohibition on commercial marketing, advertising, insurance underwriting, or individual credit assessment.&#10;&#10;### 4.3 Safe Settings&#10;- Fully air-gapped, zero-trust cloud workspaces with no outbound internet access.&#10;- Ephemeral, containerized compute instances isolated per project.&#10;- Prevention of local downloads, copy-paste exfiltration, and removable USB storage.&#10;&#10;### 4.4 Safe Data&#10;- Primary data minimization: only fields strictly necessary for the research protocol are provisioned.&#10;- Deterministic pseudonymization and separation of direct personal identifiers at source.&#10;- Multi-tier data classification (Tier 1: Aggregate/Synthetic, Tier 2: De-identified microdata, Tier 3: Controlled sensitive linkage).&#10;&#10;### 4.5 Safe Outputs&#10;- Quantitative Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) enforced on all files leaving the environment.&#10;- Mandatory primary cell suppression: any table cell with frequency fewer than 10 individuals ($k &#92;&#92;ge 10$) is suppressed.&#10;- Dominance rule masking ($p&#92;&#92;% / (n, k)$) to prevent high-contribution values from disclosing individual identities.&#10;- Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) scanning and weight inspection for machine learning model artifacts.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 5. Flow Governance Architecture (Ingress &#38; Egress)&#10;Project Wavebound regulates the continuous movement of data through policy-as-code:&#10;- **Ingress Governance (Inbound Flow)**:&#10;  - Enforces perimeter access control, Safe People accreditation, and Safe Projects ethics validation.&#10;  - Automatically verifies inbound schemas and cohort definitions against W3C SHACL shapes to prevent corrupted or unapproved datasets.&#10;- **Egress Governance (Outbound Flow)**:&#10;  - Evaluates all candidate export tables, plots, and model parameters through Open Policy Agent (OPA) Rego rules.&#10;  - Sub-millisecond execution ensures immediate fail-closed protection: if any privacy threshold is breached, export is blocked.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 6. Institutional Oversight &#38; Roles&#10;Accountability is distributed across independent roles without reliance on single-party decisions:&#10;- **Senior Information Risk Owner (SIRO)**: Executive accountability for organizational information risk and cybersecurity.&#10;- **Lead Confidentiality Guardian (Caldicott Officer)**: Independent authority safeguarding citizen confidentiality and reviewing high-tier requests.&#10;- **Data Protection Officer (DPO)**: Monitors regulatory compliance, audits Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIAs), and interfaces with the ICO.&#10;- **Independent Data Access Committee (DAC)**: Multidisciplinary panel of scientists, ethicists, and public representatives approving project access.&#10;- **Clinical Safety Officer (CSO)**: Registered clinician managing Hazard Logs and Safety Cases for algorithmic tools touching health pathways.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 7. Public Transparency &#38; Auditability&#10;- **W3C PROV-O Lineage**: Every access request, policy decision, and export clearance emits an immutable cryptographic audit record.&#10;- **Public Register of Approved Projects**: Full lay summaries of all research studies are published openly to ensure civic transparency.&#10;- **National Opt-Out Respect**: The platform enforces statutory opt-outs (including the NHS National Data Opt-Out in England), ensuring that individuals who opt out are systematically excluded from secondary research linkage.&#10;&#10;*Published by the Data Governance &#38; Access Oversight Authority.* | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

Operational Five Safes Framework&#10;Every analytical project is partitioned and controlled across the Five Safes:&#10;&#10;### 4.1 Safe People&#10;- Mandatory accreditation by statutory bodies (e.g., UKSA Research Accreditation Panel [RAP] or equivalent).&#10;- Verified institutional identity and background checks.&#10;- Annual certification in Information Governance (IG), data protection, and disclosure control methods.&#10;&#10;### 4.2 Safe Projects&#10;- Demonstrable public interest benefit test evaluated by an independent review committee.&#10;- Validated ethics clearances (NHS REC, university ethics, or statutory research panels).&#10;- Clear purpose limitation: use of data is restricted exclusively to the approved research question.&#10;- Strict prohibition on commercial marketing, advertising, insurance underwriting, or individual credit assessment.&#10;&#10;### 4.3 Safe Settings&#10;- Fully air-gapped, zero-trust cloud workspaces with no outbound internet access.&#10;- Ephemeral, containerized compute instances isolated per project.&#10;- Prevention of local downloads, copy-paste exfiltration, and removable USB storage.&#10;&#10;### 4.4 Safe Data&#10;- Primary data minimization: only fields strictly necessary for the research protocol are provisioned.&#10;- Deterministic pseudonymization and separation of direct personal identifiers at source.&#10;- Multi-tier data classification (Tier 1: Aggregate/Synthetic, Tier 2: De-identified microdata, Tier 3: Controlled sensitive linkage).&#10;&#10;### 4.5 Safe Outputs&#10;- Quantitative Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) enforced on all files leaving the environment.&#10;- Mandatory primary cell suppression: any table cell with frequency fewer than 10 individuals ($k &#92;&#92;ge 10$) is suppressed.&#10;- Dominance rule masking ($p&#92;&#92;% / (n, k)$) to prevent high-contribution values from disclosing individual identities.&#10;- Abstract Syntax Tree (AST) scanning and weight inspection for machine learning model artifacts.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 5. Flow Governance Architecture (Ingress &#38; Egress)&#10;Project Seawater regulates the continuous movement of data through policy-as-code:&#10;- **Ingress Governance (Inbound Flow)**:&#10;  - Enforces perimeter access control, Safe People accreditation, and Safe Projects ethics validation.&#10;  - Automatically verifies inbound schemas and cohort definitions against W3C SHACL shapes to prevent corrupted or unapproved datasets.&#10;- **Egress Governance (Outbound Flow)**:&#10;  - Evaluates all candidate export tables, plots, and model parameters through Open Policy Agent (OPA) Rego rules.&#10;  - Sub-millisecond execution ensures immediate fail-closed protection: if any privacy threshold is breached, export is blocked.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 6. Institutional Oversight &#38; Roles&#10;Accountability is distributed across independent roles without reliance on single-party decisions:&#10;- **Senior Information Risk Owner (SIRO)**: Executive accountability for organizational information risk and cybersecurity.&#10;- **Lead Confidentiality Guardian (Caldicott Officer)**: Independent authority safeguarding citizen confidentiality and reviewing high-tier requests.&#10;- **Data Protection Officer (DPO)**: Monitors regulatory compliance, audits Data Protection Impact Assessments (DPIAs), and interfaces with the ICO.&#10;- **Independent Data Access Committee (DAC)**: Multidisciplinary panel of scientists, ethicists, and public representatives approving project access.&#10;- **Clinical Safety Officer (CSO)**: Registered clinician managing Hazard Logs and Safety Cases for algorithmic tools touching health pathways.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 7. Public Transparency &#38; Auditability&#10;- **W3C PROV-O Lineage**: Every access request, policy decision, and export clearance emits an immutable cryptographic audit record.&#10;- **Public Register of Approved Projects**: Full lay summaries of all research studies are published openly to ensure civic transparency.&#10;- **National Opt-Out Respect**: The platform enforces statutory opt-outs (including the NHS National Data Opt-Out in England), ensuring that individuals who opt out are systematically excluded from secondary research linkage.&#10;&#10;*Published by the Data Governance &#38; Access Oversight Authority.* | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
| C-666b2e3133fc840a2e6f-1-1 | [src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx](../src/components/GovernanceAtlas.tsx#L244) | 244 | # PUBLIC INTEREST DATA GOVERNANCE &#38; POPULATION RESEARCH CHARTER&#10;*Standard Operating Protocol &#38; Statutory Compliance Specification for Data Analysis &#38; Intelligence Platforms*&#10;*Document Reference: WAVEBOUND-PAC-GOV-2026-V4 &#124; Authorized by the Data Governance &#38; Ethics Oversight Committee*&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 1. Purpose &#38; Scope&#10;This Charter establishes the mandatory governance policy, ethical safeguards, and machine-executable controls for modern data analysis and intelligence platforms (including Trusted Research Environments [TREs], Secure Data Environments [SDEs], and AI sandbox enclaves). &#10;&#10;The platform supports high-impact research across population data science, epidemiology, public health, demographic analysis, social statistics, and approved commercial health life-sciences partnerships. All analytical workloads operate under an overarching principle: data subjects and citizens must remain completely protected against individual re-identification, surveillance, or unauthorized exploitation.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 2. Statutory Mandates &#38; Legal Processing Gateways&#10;Processing across this platform is authorized and governed under statutory legislation:&#10;- **Digital Economy Act 2017 (Part 5, Chapter 5: Research)**:&#10;  - Section 64: Disclosure of public authority data for research in the public interest.&#10;  - Section 70 &#38; 71: Statutory Code of Practice and accreditation of processors, environments, and researchers under UK Statistics Authority (UKSA) oversight.&#10;  - Section 67: Strict criminal penalties for unlawful or unauthorized disclosure of identifying data.&#10;- **UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR)**:&#10;  - Article 5: Principles of data minimization, purpose limitation, integrity, and confidentiality.&#10;  - Article 6(1)(e): Processing is necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 9(2)(j): Processing of special category data is lawful for archiving and scientific research in the public interest.&#10;  - Article 89(1): Technical and organizational safeguards for research and statistical analysis.&#10;- **Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA 2018)**: Part 2 statutory provisions and Section 19 research safeguards.&#10;- **Statistics and Registration Service Act 2007 (SRSA 2007)**: Authorizing statistical research and linkage under public interest mandates.&#10;- **National Health Service Act 2006 (Section 251)**: Statutory exemption granted on advice of the Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG) where clinical data linkage is required without consent.&#10;- **Common Law Duty of Confidentiality &#38; The Eight Caldicott Principles**: Ensuring that duty to share for public benefit is balanced with strict confidentiality.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 3. Standards Architecture &#38; Quality Frameworks&#10;The platform compiles and verifies compliance against national and international standards:&#10;- **ISO/IEC 42001:2023**: Artificial Intelligence Management System (AIMS) governing predictive models and in-enclave machine learning.&#10;- **ISO/IEC 27001:2022 &#38; ISO/IEC 27701:2019**: Information Security and Privacy Information Management Systems (ISMS/PIMS).&#10;- **ISO/IEC 5259 Series**: Data quality and bias minimization standards for machine learning and analytical pipelines.&#10;- **DCB0129 &#38; DCB0160**: Statutory clinical risk management standards for health software manufacturers and deploying health organizations.&#10;- **UK Medical Devices Regulations 2002 &#38; MHRA SaMD**: Design and risk controls for Software as a Medical Device developed within enclaves.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## 4. Operational Five Safes Framework&#10;Every analytical project is partitioned and controlled across the Five Safes:&#10;&#10;### 4.1 Safe People&#10;- Mandatory accreditation by statutory bodies (e.g., UKSA Research Accreditation Panel [RAP] or equivalent).&#10;- Verified institutional identity and background checks.&#10;- Annual certification in Information Governance (IG), data protection, and disclosure control methods.&#10;&#10;### 4.2 Safe Projects&#10;- Demonstrable public interest benefit test evaluated by an independent review committee.&#10;- Validated ethics clearances (NHS REC, university ethics, or statutory research panels).&#10;- Clear purpose limitation: use of data is restricted exclusively to the approved research question.&#10;- Strict prohibition on commercial marketing, advertising, insurance underwriting, or individual credit assessment.&#10;&#10;### 4.3 Safe Settings&#10;- Fully air-gapped, zero-trust cloud workspaces with no outbound internet access.&#10;- Ephemeral, containerized compute instances isolated per project.&#10;- Prevention of local downloads, copy-paste exfiltration, and removable USB storage.&#10;&#10;### 4.4 Safe Data&#10;- Primary data minimization: only fields strictly necessary for the research protocol are provisioned.&#10;- Deterministic pseudonymization and separation of direct personal identifiers at source.&#10;- | C-a2f04146cafc759606cc-1-1 | [src/components/IGQuickGuide.tsx](../src/components/IGQuickGuide.tsx#L374) | 374 | # Universal Research Ethics &#38; Data Governance Planning Proforma&#10;A comprehensive, multi-agency planning dossier for research protocols accessing population health datasets across safe data intelligence environments.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 1: STUDY IDENTIFICATION, FUNDING &#38; INVESTIGATOR TEAM&#10;&#10;1.1 Study Full Title: [Insert Full Scientific Title, e.g. Evaluating Socioeconomic Disparities in Post-Myocardial Infarction Outcomes: A Multi-Center Longitudinal Linkage Study]&#10;1.2 Short Title / Study Acronym: [e.g. CARDIO-DISPARITY-UK]&#10;1.3 Plain-English Lay Summary (Max 300 words for Public &#38; Patient Consumption):&#10;    [Provide an accessible, non-technical overview explaining: What is the clinical problem? Why is linked patient data essential? What will change for patients or NHS care as a result of this work? Avoid clinical acronyms; write for a general public audience.]&#10;1.4 Research Funding &#38; Grant Award Information:&#10;    - Primary Funding Body / Grant Agency: [e.g. National Institute for Health and Care Research (NIHR) / Medical Research Council (MRC) / Wellcome Trust / British Heart Foundation]&#10;    - Grant / Award Reference Number: [e.g. NIHR-HTA-142890]&#10;    - Total Grant Award (£ GBP): [e.g. £842,500]&#10;    - Funding Duration: [e.g. 36 Months: 01 Oct 2024 to 30 Sep 2027]&#10;    - Funder Type: [X] Non-commercial Public / Charity   [ ] Commercial Industry Sponsored&#10;    - Financial Conflict of Interest Statement: [Confirm no financial incentives tied to study outcomes]&#10;1.5 Target Ethics, Statutory &#38; Data Governance Approval Bodies:&#10;    [X] HRA Research Ethics Committee (NHS REC)&#10;    [X] Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG Section 251) - for unconsented secondary care linkage&#10;    [X] Data Access Committee (DAC / IGRP / Information Governance Panel)&#10;    [X] National Data Opt-Out (NDOO) Exemption / Compliance Verification&#10;1.6 Chief Investigator (CI) &#38; Professional Affiliations:&#10;    - Name: [Prof. / Dr. Jane Doe]&#10;    - Position &#38; Department: [Professor of Clinical Epidemiology, Faculty of Medicine]&#10;    - Sponsoring / Submitting Institution: [Accredited UK University / NHS Foundation Trust Legal Entity]&#10;    - Professional Network Affiliations: [e.g. British Cardiovascular Society (BCS), Royal College of Physicians (RCP), Society for Social Medicine &#38; Population Health, HDR UK Fellow]&#10;    - Statutory Clinical Registration (if applicable): [GMC / NMC / GPhC Registration Number: 1234567]&#10;    - ONS / UKSA Safe Researcher Accreditation Number: [e.g. ONS-SR-994821; Expiry: 14/10/2027]&#10;    - Mandatory NHS Information Governance Certification: [NHS DSPT Certificate / MRC Research Governance, Date: DD/MM/YYYY]&#10;1.7 Co-Investigators, Analysts &#38; Collaborative Consortia:&#10;    - [List all named personnel with institutional affiliations, orcid IDs, Safe Researcher accreditation numbers, and specific analytical responsibilities]&#10;    - Partner Consortia: [e.g. NHS Integrated Care Board (ICB) Data Partnership, HDR UK National Driver Programme]&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 2: CLINICAL RATIONALE, STAKEHOLDER BENEFIT &#38; PPIE CO-DESIGN (CALDICOTT PRINCIPLE 1)&#10;&#10;2.1 Scientific Background &#38; Unmet Need:&#10;    [Explain the clinical or epidemiological knowledge gap, population disease burden, and clinical rationale for study]&#10;2.2 Clear Statement of Public &#38; Health System Benefit:&#10;    - Direct Clinical / Health Improvements: [e.g. Identification of clinical touchpoints to reduce 30-day readmissions by 12%]&#10;    - Health Equity &#38; Resource Allocation: [e.g. Optimizing secondary prevention care pathways in high-deprivation areas]&#10;2.3 Benefit Realization Pathways (Categorize Primary Mechanism):&#10;    - [X] **Scientific &#38; Epidemiological Knowledge**: Generating peer-reviewed open-access clinical evidence, risk stratification models, and updating NICE clinical guidelines.&#10;    - [X] **Health System Operational Improvement**: Providing NHS Integrated Care Boards (ICBs) with capacity planning benchmarks, service pathway audits, and hospital bed-day reduction targets.&#10;    - [ ] **Person-Level / Direct Clinical Intervention**: (Note: Observational TRE research rarely feeds into direct individual care; if claimed, specify clinical feedback protocols and GMC duty of care).&#10;2.4 Patient and Public Involvement and Engagement (PPIE) Co-Design:&#10;    - PPIE Advisory Panel Composition: [e.g. 8 patient representatives with lived experience of acute coronary syndrome, recruited via British Heart Foundation Patient Voice and local ICB patient networks].&#10;    - How PPIE Influenced the Protocol: [Detail specific adjustments made following patient feedback, e.g. panel requested explicit inclusion of mental health comorbidity variables and advised on acceptable aggregation levels].&#10;    - Ongoing PPIE Role: [e.g. Co-designing lay summaries, co-presenting webinar findings to community groups, and reviewing candidate airlock publication tables for stigmatization risks].&#10;2.5 Commercial &#38; Secondary Use Restrictions:&#10;    - Affirmation: Research is strictly public interest. Under no circumstances will cohort data or microdata findings be shared with commercial insurers for underwriting, employers for vetting, or credit rating agencies.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 3: POPULATION COHORT DEFINITIONS &#38; ELIGIBILITY CRITERIA&#10;&#10;3.1 Cohort Inclusion Criteria:&#10;    - Age Range: Adults aged &#62;= 18 years at date of index event (binned in 5-year strata for analysis).&#10;    - Clinical Phenotype &#38; Index Diagnosis:&#10;      * Primary Hospital Admission for Acute Coronary Syndrome (ICD-10 codes: I20.0, I21.0-I21.9, I22.0-I22.9)&#10;      * OR verified Primary Care SNOMED CT clinical concept terms (e.g. Concept ID: 22298006 'Myocardial infarction')&#10;    - Time Period / Observation Window:&#10;      * Index Inpatient Window: 01 January 2016 through 31 December 2022&#10;      * Baseline Pre-Index Comorbidity Lookback: 5 years prior to index admission&#10;      * Post-Index Longitudinal Follow-up Horizon: Up to 31 December 2024 or date of death&#10;3.2 Cohort Exclusion Criteria:&#10;    - Non-residents without valid NHS digital service area coverage.&#10;    - Patients with documented historical National Data Opt-Out (NDOO) preference flags at extraction date.&#10;    - Temporary, overseas, or unlinked administrative encounter records.&#10;3.3 Demographic &#38; Social Stratification Definitions:&#10;    - Demographics: Biological sex at birth; age (5-year bands: 18-24, 25-29, 30-34... 85+); self-reported ethnicity (ONS 18+1 category classification collapsed into 5 high-level census groups if cell counts &#60; 10).&#10;    - Socioeconomic Deprivation: Index of Multiple Deprivation (IMD) national deciles (1 = most deprived, 10 = least deprived) derived at Lower Layer Super Output Area (LSOA) level. Individual street postcodes are strictly excluded.&#10;    - Geographic Bounding: Regional NHS Integrated Care Board (ICB) boundaries or Government Office Regions (GOR).&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 4: MULTI-DATASET SPECIFICATION &#38; GRANULAR VARIABLE CATALOG&#10;&#10;&#124; Dataset Identifier &#124; Custodian / National Source &#124; Observation Horizon &#124; Granular Variables Requested &#124; Caldicott Principle 3 Justification (Why Necessary &#38; Minimal) &#124;&#10;&#124; :--- &#124; :--- &#124; :--- &#124; :--- &#124; :--- &#124;&#10;&#124; **Dataset A: Secondary Care Inpatient (HES / SUS / SMR01)** &#124; NHS England / NHS Scotland &#124; 2011-01-01 to 2022-12-31 &#124; - Pseudonymised Study ID&#60;br&#62;- Admission Year &#38; Quarter&#60;br&#62;- Length of Stay (days)&#60;br&#62;- Primary ICD-10 diagnosis (3-digit)&#60;br&#62;- Secondary ICD-10 comorbidities (Charlson Index)&#60;br&#62;- OPCS-4 surgical intervention codes&#60;br&#62;- Admission method (elective vs emergency)&#60;br&#62;- Discharge destination (home, care facility, death) &#124; Essential to establish acute index event, procedural revascularisation (PCI/CABG), and major adverse cardiovascular events (MACE). Exact admission dates suppressed; year/quarter suffices. &#124;&#10;&#124; **Dataset B: Primary Care Electronic Health Records (GP Encounters)** &#124; GP Practice Networks / CPRD / QResearch &#124; 2011-01-01 to 2024-12-31 &#124; - Pseudonymised Study ID&#60;br&#62;- Consultation Year &#38; Month&#60;br&#62;- SNOMED CT codes for hypertension, diabetes, smoking status&#60;br&#62;- Systolic / Diastolic Blood Pressure (mmHg)&#60;br&#62;- Body Mass Index (BMI category)&#60;br&#62;- Serum Cholesterol / eGFR lab values (binned) &#124; Required to control for pre-existing baseline metabolic risk factors and secondary prevention follow-up in primary care. Exact clinic appointment timestamps suppressed. &#124;&#10;&#124; **Dataset C: Community Prescribing &#38; Dispensing (ePACT2 / PIS)** &#124; NHS Business Services Authority (NHSBSA) &#124; 2015-01-01 to 2024-12-31 &#124; - Pseudonymised Study ID&#60;br&#62;- BNF Chemical Chapter 2 (Cardiovascular): Statins, ACE inhibitors, Beta-blockers, Dual antiplatelet therapy&#60;br&#62;- Prescription Year &#38; Quarter&#60;br&#62;- Total quantity dispensed &#124; Tracks post-discharge guideline-directed medical therapy compliance. Non-cardiovascular prescription chapters are excluded to enforce data minimisation. &#124;&#10;&#124; **Dataset D: National Mortality Civil Registrations (ONS / NRS)** &#124; Office for National Statistics (ONS) &#124; 2016-01-01 to 2024-12-31 &#124; - Pseudonymised Study ID&#60;br&#62;- Year &#38; Month of Death&#60;br&#62;- Underlying Cause of Death (ICD-10 4-digit)&#60;br&#62;- Contributing Secondary Causes (ICD-10)&#60;br&#62;- Place of Death (Hospital, Care Home, Hospice, Domestic) &#124; Primary clinical endpoint: all-cause mortality and cardiovascular-specific mortality within 30 days, 1 year, and 5 years. Exact date of death suppressed; interval days to death used. &#124;&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 5: EXTERNAL DATASETS TO BE BROUGHT IN (INGRESS PIPELINE)&#10;&#10;5.1 External Reference Datasets Requested for Ingress:&#10;    - [e.g. DEFRA Air Quality Urban Ambient Particulate Matter (PM2.5 / NO2) Annual Grids at 1km² resolution]&#10;    - [e.g. ONS Census 2021 Area Classification for Output Areas (OAC) / Rural-Urban Classification]&#10;    - [e.g. Public Health England Active Lives Physical Activity Survey Index]&#10;5.2 Ingress Governance &#38; Copyright Checks:&#10;    - Confirm all external datasets are published under Open Government Licence (OGL v3.0) or possess explicit third-party data licensing agreements.&#10;    - Confirm NO external patient-level microdata containing direct identifiers will be imported without prior DAC and honest broker approval.&#10;    - Aggregation Level: External attributes will be linked strictly at geographic area level (LSOA / MSOA centroid or 1km grid) to prevent triangulated reverse identification.&#10;5.3 Ingress Sanitisation &#38; Inspection Protocol:&#10;    - All incoming external data files pass through the TRE Ingress Airlock to verify checksums (SHA-256), scan for malicious macros, and ensure zero embedded row-level personal data.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 6: STATUTORY LAWFUL BASIS &#38; CONFIDENTIALITY SAFEGUARDS&#10;&#10;6.1 UK General Data Protection Regulation (UK GDPR):&#10;    - Article 6 Condition: Article 6(1)(e) - Necessary for the performance of a task carried out in the public interest.&#10;    - Article 9 Condition (Special Category Health Data): Article 9(2)(j) - Processing is necessary for archiving purposes in the public interest, scientific or historical research purposes in accordance with Section 19 of the Data Protection Act 2018.&#10;6.2 Data Protection Act 2018 (Section 19 Research Safeguards):&#10;    - Confirmation that processing will NOT cause substantial damage or substantial distress to any data subject.&#10;    - Processing will NOT be used to support decisions or measures regarding any specific individual.&#10;    - Technical and organisational safeguards (pseudonymisation, air-gapped enclaves) are actively applied.&#10;6.3 Common Law Duty of Confidentiality (CLDC):&#10;    - Gateway Selected: [ ] Explicit Informed Consent  [X] Section 251 Exemption (NHS Act 2006)&#10;    - If Section 251: Confidentiality Advisory Group (CAG) Reference: [Insert Pending or Approved CAG Ref]&#10;    - The Feasibility / Impracticability Test:&#10;      * Cohort volume (n &#62; 150,000) spans multi-centre hospital episodes over 8 years.&#10;      * High mortality rate (~24% deceased by study end), making individual re-consent impossible.&#10;      * Selection bias: Seeking consent from surviving, contactable patients would systematically under-represent acute, fatal presentations, introducing catastrophic epidemiological bias.&#10;6.4 National Data Opt-Out (NDOO) Policy Compliance:&#10;    - Data custodian applies NHS NDOO algorithm to purge opted-out patient records prior to honest broker linkage and enclave provisioning.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 7: ENCLAVE TECHNICAL ENVIRONMENT &#38; ACCESS SAFEGUARDS (SAFE SETTING)&#10;&#10;7.1 Target Enclave Specification:&#10;    - Accredited Trusted Research Environment (TRE) / Secure Data Environment (SDE) conforming to SATRE v1.1, NHS DSPT, and ISO/IEC 27001.&#10;    - Network Architecture: Zero-Egress VPC; default outbound internet gateway routes permanently severed.&#10;    - Client Boundary: Secure Virtual Desktop Infrastructure (VDI); local file transfer, clipboard copy-paste, and external device mapping permanently disabled.&#10;7.2 Pseudonymisation &#38; Honest Brokerage:&#10;    - Direct identifiers (NHS numbers, full names, addresses) are stripped by custodians before ingest.&#10;    - Linkage performed via salted one-way HMAC tokens generated by an independent honest broker.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 8: STATISTICAL DISCLOSURE CONTROL (SDC) &#38; AIRLOCK EGRESS AGREEMENT (SAFE OUTPUT)&#10;&#10;8.1 Output Checking Standards:&#10;    - Primary Cell Suppression: Any tabular frequency count representing fewer than 10 individuals (k &#60; 10) is masked.&#10;    - Dominance Rule: No single contributing individual or practice may account for &#62;= 80% of any summary value.&#10;    - Secondary (Complementary) Suppression: Neighboring cells are suppressed to prevent row/column subtraction deduction.&#10;    - Graphical Visualisations: Scatter plots must be rendered as 2D hexbins or kernel density contours; individual outlier coordinates are prohibited; survival curves must truncate right-censored tails when at-risk patient numbers fall below 10.&#10;8.2 Two-Person Review Protocol:&#10;    - All candidate outputs undergo automated mathematical rule checks followed by independent inspection by two certified Safe Output Reviewers.&#10;&#10;---&#10;&#10;## SECTION 9: DISSEMINATION, KNOWLEDGE TRANSLATION &#38; OPEN SCIENCE&#10;&#10;9.1 Academic &#38; Clinical Dissemination:&#10;    - Peer-Reviewed Open Access Publications: Findings targeted at leading high-impact clinical and epidemiological journals conforming to UKRI Open Access policies.&#10;    - Clinical Practice Guidelines: Formal synthesis reports submitted to NICE, British Cardiovascular Society, and NHS England Clinical Reference Groups.&#10;9.2 Public &#38; Patient Knowledge Translation:&#10;    - Plain-English Summary Pamphlets: Co-authored with the PPIE Advisory Panel and published on public university and NHS Trust portals.&#10;    - Community Webinars &#38; Interactive Infographics: Hosting accessible public briefings explaining study insights without statistical jargon.&#10;9.3 Code &#38; Methodological Transparency:&#10;    - Analytical Code Release: All data-cleaning scripts, statistical models (R/Python), and SHACL/Rego policy configurations will be deposited in an open GitHub repository under an MIT License (containing zero patient data).&#10;    - Metadata Registry: Study protocol and Data Use Register (DUR) entries registered on the Health Data Research Innovation Gateway (HDR UK Gateway).&#10;9.4 Legal Undertaking:&#10;    - The Chief Investigator and all named researchers formally commit NOT to attempt re-identification, reverse linkage, or unauthorized disclosure under statutory penalties of the Digital Economy Act 2017 Section 67 and the Data Protection Act 2018 Section 171. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

APPLICANT &#38; STATUTORY GATEWAY&#10;- Applicant: [Chief Investigator, Department, Institutional Host]&#10;- Statutory Basis: Section 251 of the National Health Service Act 2006.&#10;- Purpose: Medical research in the public interest where patient consent is impracticable.&#10;&#10;## 2. THE FEASIBILITY &#38; IMPRACTICABILITY TEST (WHY CONSENT CANNOT BE OBTAINED)&#10;2.1 Cohort Magnitude: [e.g. 185,000 patient episodes across 42 acute hospital trusts].&#10;2.2 Historical Cohort &#38; Deceased Rate:&#10;    - The cohort spans historical records from 2012 to 2022.&#10;    - An estimated [32]% of the target cohort are deceased, making re-consent legally and factually impossible.&#10;2.3 Fatal Epidemiological Selection Bias:&#10;    - Patients presenting with severe, acute, or sudden-onset conditions have high early mortality and morbidity.&#10;    - Requiring active opt-in consent would disproportionately skew the cohort toward surviving, healthier, or less socioeconomically deprived patients, invalidating research generalisability.&#10;2.4 Patient Distress &#38; Clinical Burden:&#10;    - Contacting hundreds of thousands of individuals or bereaved relatives years after acute emergency admissions causes unwarranted distress.&#10;&#10;## 3. NATIONAL DATA OPT-OUT (NDOO) &#38; PATIENT TRANSPARENCY&#10;4.1 NDOO Application:&#10;    - Data custodians will filter out all patients registered with the NHS National Data Opt-Out prior to dataset extraction.&#10;4.2 Fair Processing / Transparency:&#10;    - Project transparency notices will be displayed on participating NHS Trust websites with dedicated contact points for opt-out queries. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-d076bef78ae2933cafc7-1-1 | [src/components/ImplementationTrack.tsx](../src/components/ImplementationTrack.tsx#L936) | 936 | name: Seawater Policy-as-Code CI&#10;on:&#10;  push:&#10;    branches: [ main ]&#10;  pull_request:&#10;    branches: [ main ]&#10;&#10;jobs:&#10;  validate-and-compile:&#10;    runs-on: ubuntu-latest&#10;    steps:&#10;      - uses: actions/checkout@v4&#10;&#10;      - name: Set up Python 3.11&#10;        uses: actions/setup-python@v5&#10;        with:&#10;          python-version: "3.11"&#10;          cache: "pip"&#10;&#10;      - name: Install dependencies&#10;        run: &#124;&#10;          pip install -r requirements.txt&#10;&#10;      - name: Step 1: Lint RDF/Turtle Ontologies&#10;        run: &#124;&#10;          python -c "&#10;          import rdflib, glob&#10;          for f in glob.glob('ontology/**/*.ttl', recursive=True):&#10;              g = rdflib.Graph()&#10;              g.parse(f, format='turtle')&#10;              print(f'OK: {f}')&#10;          "&#10;&#10;      - name: Step 2: Run PySHACL Closed-World Test Harness&#10;        run: &#124;&#10;          pytest tests/test_shapes.py -v --junitxml=reports/pyshacl-results.xml&#10;&#10;      - name: Step 3: Compile Semantic Graph to OPA Rego&#10;        run: &#124;&#10;          python compiler/rdf_to_rego.py --input ontology/ --output security/opa/authz.rego&#10;&#10;      - name: Step 4: Run OPA Rego Unit Tests&#10;        run: &#124;&#10;          curl -L -o opa https://openpolicyagent.org/downloads/latest/opa_linux_amd64&#10;          chmod +x opa&#10;          ./opa test security/opa/ -v | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-c6a4a13c5970a72f6769-1-1 | [src/components/PlaybookReader.tsx](../src/components/PlaybookReader.tsx#L50) | 50 | • The Seawater Maritime Problem Space &#38; Tidal Navigation: Clinical and genomic research operates not within a static quarantine pond, but across an open, turbulent maritime problem space dubbed "Seawater". Vessels—ranging from light exploratory query scripts to massive foundation model training clusters—must navigate shifting regulatory channels, unpredictable swells of adversarial prompt injections, and hidden underwater reefs of de-anonymization. The tide flows cyclically: the incoming flood tide brings massive surges of EHR, multi-omics, imaging, and registry microdata (ingress), while the receding ebb tide carries out derived statistical aggregates, trained model weights, and clinical discoveries (egress). Without active harbor defenses, salinity gatekeepers (W3C SHACL shape validation), and algorithmic sea-locks (microsecond OPA Rego policies), the enclave suffers either stagnation behind impassable bureaucratic drydocks or catastrophic contamination and leakage out into the open ocean. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

The Classic Infrastructure Trap: Traditional IT configurations mistakenly hardcode governance rules directly into cluster network rules or pod name whitelists (e.g. "allow IP 10.244.2.14 to access clinical database X"). When a container crashes, restarts, or migrates to a different worker node, the IP changes—causing immediate outages or leaving behind orphaned, unverified permissions. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

Proposed Integration Pattern: An admission service could evaluate trusted researcher and project claims against approved policy rules derived from reviewed governance assertions. A graph statement alone is not permission: the organisation must define all relevant conditions, freshness checks, failure behaviour and enforcement tests. This integration is not implemented in the current application. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-3cda61ddcd26e2a8a34a-1-1 | [src/components/SDCMethodsGuide.tsx](../src/components/SDCMethodsGuide.tsx#L100) | 100 | # Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) &#38; Low-Count Analytical Guide&#10;&#10;A practitioner reference standard for population data research organizations, epidemiologists, data stewards, and governance committees.&#10;&#10;## 1. Small Cell Disclosure Risks (N &#60; 5 or N &#60; 10)&#10;When presenting aggregate tables or public reports containing small cell counts, specific disclosure control methods prevent re-identification through direct identification or indirect deduction.&#10;&#10;## 2. Core SDC Techniques&#10;- Cell Suppression: Primary (redacting N &#60; 10) and Secondary / Complementary Suppression (masking totals/adjacent cells to prevent subtraction deduction).&#10;- Data Aggregation: Spatial Aggregation (counties/regions instead of postal districts), Temporal Aggregation (3- or 5-year moving averages), Demographic Recoding (broader age bands, top-level ICD chapters).&#10;- Data Perturbation &#38; Noise: Targeted / Controlled Rounding (base 3 or 5), Random Swapping / PRAM, and Differential Privacy (Laplace / Gaussian calibrated noise).&#10;&#10;## 3. Methodological Approaches for Low-Count Data&#10;- Bayesian Hierarchical Modeling: Borrowing strength via Empirical Bayes and Spatial Conditional Autoregressive (CAR) models to smooth rate volatility.&#10;- International Consortia &#38; Multi-Center Registries: Standardized protocols pooling rare condition cohorts globally with uniform governance.&#10;- Qualitative &#38; Mixed-Methods Frameworks: Structured case series, process tracing, and Qualitative Comparative Analysis (QCA) when N &#60; 30.&#10;&#10;## 4. Data Governance &#38; Safe Access Models&#10;- Five Safes Framework: Safe People, Safe Projects, Safe Settings, Safe Data, Safe Outputs.&#10;- Secure Data Enclaves: Zero-egress sovereign analytical environments.&#10;- Output Checking: Independent disclosure control clearance before external release. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-18acb14daf34a9344c6d-1-1 | [src/data/agenticGovernanceData.ts](../src/data/agenticGovernanceData.ts#L84) | 84 | Under statutory frameworks (UK Digital Economy Act 2017, ONS Approved Researcher Scheme, Caldicott Principles, and UK GDPR), a "Safe Person" must possess legal personhood, personal duty of confidentiality, and direct criminal/civil liability. An AI model, agent, or LLM possesses no legal personhood, cannot sign statutory undertakings, cannot hold institutional liability, and cannot be prosecuted. &#10;&#10;Therefore, AI operates strictly as an Agentic Workload Entity under the Delegated Authority of an Accredited Human Researcher ("Human Sponsor"). Following the Stanford UIT Agentic AI Security Framework, the AI's identity must be an automated, near-instantaneously revocable machine workload credential tied to the sponsor's verified identity.&#10;&#10;Simultaneously, pre-trained weights, fine-tuned checkpoints, embeddings, and vector databases are formally categorized as "Data Assets" within the Safe Data pillar. Because large language models and neural networks are susceptible to training data memorization, membership inference, and model inversion attacks, exporting a model artifact requires the exact same statistical disclosure control (SDC) and airlock verification as exporting a tabular dataset. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-9a25621a8fe35ca79292-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L436) | 436 | Indirect identifying variables—including appointment timestamps, hospital admission/discharge dates, birth year/month, partial postcode districts (e.g. SW1A), sex, ethnicity, and rare clinical diagnosis codes. Sweeney’s landmark theorem demonstrated that 87% of the US/UK population is uniquely identifiable by just 5-digit ZIP code/postcode, gender, and date of birth. In healthcare research, removing patient names does not confer true anonymity: linking multiple quasi-identifiers against voter registries, local news, or obituary notices can immediately re-identify patients. Under UK GDPR and Caldicott Principle 1, such row-level datasets remain confidential personal data requiring strict airlock suppression, k-anonymity grouping, and AST scrubbing. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-ef8eed4917fa9e509aeb-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L452) | 452 | # Common SDC Rules &#38; Analytical Applications Matrix:&#10;1. Primary Cell Suppression: k &#62;= 10 (or k &#62;= 5). Any count between 1 and k-1 is suppressed.&#10;2. Dominance / (n, k) Rule: Top 1 contributor &#60;= 50%, top 2 contributors &#60;= 80% of aggregate sum.&#10;3. Quasi-Identifiers: Dates of birth, admission/discharge timestamps, partial postcodes, and rare diagnoses form high-dimensional fingerprints that re-identify individuals even without names.&#10;4. Logistic Regression: Suppress extreme odds ratios and parameter estimates where contingency cells are sparse (preventing quasi-complete separation disclosure).&#10;5. Survival Analysis (Kaplan-Meier): Smooth or truncate survival curves when the active risk set falls below 10; suppress individual tick marks for censored patients.&#10;6. Continuous Statistics: Suppress true minimums and maximums; replace with 5th and 95th percentiles. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

In a safe digital enclave, automated GitOps scripts synthesize and place these files in repository roots to prevent accidental ingestion of data directories (&#96;/data/&#96;, &#96;*.parquet&#96;, &#96;*.csv&#96;) into prompt context windows.&#92;n&#92;nArchitectural Reference Note: Client-side ignore files are an advisory developer heuristic, NOT a security guarantee. Subprocesses, user shell overrides, or extension parser bugs can bypass client filters. Consequently, trusted research enclaves must back ignore files with non-bypassable host controls: read-only POSIX directory permissions (chmod 400), isolated storage subnets without WAN routing, and pre-prompt AST token proxy inspection. | requirement or control claim | active | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
| C-3ce5445445af17d26ba5-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L705) | 705 | Clinical and genomic research takes place within an active, continuous data problem space represented by seawater. Analytics workloads (ranging from exploratory query scripts to distributed foundation model pipelines) must navigate regulatory requirements, clinical governance channels, and patient confidentiality thresholds. The tide flows cyclically across two managed boundaries: Ingress and Egress. Ingress enforces gateway access controls, researcher accreditation, institutional identity verification, and Information Governance training (Safe People), alongside approved REC ethics protocols and Section 251 CAG exemptions (Safe Projects). Inbound clinical datasets, multi-omics records, and container workloads are verified at the boundary using W3C SHACL shape validation before admission. Egress regulates the export of derived analytical findings, statistical summaries, and model parameters, requiring automated Statistical Disclosure Control (SDC) checks, k-anonymity (k &#62;= 10), and sub-millisecond Open Policy Agent (OPA) Rego policy evaluations to prevent patient re-identification. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |

| C-74cbbbcf290f5b8f0356-1-1 | [src/data/glossaryData.ts](../src/data/glossaryData.ts#L715) | 715 | Clinical and genomic research takes place not in static isolated ponds, but in an open, turbulent maritime problem space dubbed "Seawater". Vessels—ranging from light exploratory scripts to heavy multi-node foundation model training runs—must navigate through shifting regulatory channels, unpredictable swells of adversarial prompt injections, and deep underwater hazards of de-anonymization. The tide flows cyclically: the flood tide brings in massive volumes of EHR, genomics, and registry microdata (ingress), while the receding ebb tide carries out derived statistical aggregates, trained model weights, and research publications (egress). Without active harbor defenses, salinity gatekeepers (W3C SHACL shape validation), and algorithmic sea-locks (microsecond OPA Rego policies), the enclave suffers either stagnation behind impassable bureaucratic drydocks or catastrophic contamination and leakage out into the open ocean. | requirement or control claim | absent | unreviewed |  |  |  |  |
